<a id='question-146'></a>

### Question 146: Immunodeficiency & Opportunistic Infection
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the ability to identify the specific type of immunodeficiency based on the pattern of opportunistic infections and the underlying cellular/molecular defect, specifically focusing on defects in T-cell development and function, leading to susceptibility to intracellular pathogens and graft rejection. (考察免疫缺陷的类型，根据机会性感染模式和潜在的细胞/分子缺陷来识别，特别是T细胞发育和功能缺陷，导致对细胞内病原体和移植物排斥的易感性。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 3-year-old boy presents with recurrent severe infections, including Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia (PJP), chronic diarrhea caused by Cryptosporidium parvum, and failure to thrive. He has never received any vaccinations. Physical examination reveals lymphopenia and hepatosplenomegaly. A bone marrow biopsy shows markedly reduced numbers of T lymphocytes but normal numbers of B lymphocytes and myeloid cells. Flow cytometry analysis of peripheral blood lymphocytes reveals a lack of CD3+ T cells and CD4+ helper T cells, while CD8+ cytotoxic T cells are also severely reduced. CD19+ B cells are present at normal levels. The patient's mother reports that he developed severe graft-versus-host disease (GVHD) after receiving a blood transfusion from his mother six months prior. Which of the following is the most likely underlying defect in this patient?

(A) Defective recombination activating gene (RAG) 1/2 leading to severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID) with absent B and T cells.
(B) Defective interleukin-2 receptor gamma chain (IL-2Rγc) leading to X-linked severe combined immunodeficiency (X-SCID) with absent T cells and NK cells, but normal B cells.
(C) Defective adenosine deaminase (ADA) leading to severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID) with accumulation of toxic metabolites affecting lymphocyte development.
(D) Defective CD40 ligand (CD40L) leading to X-linked hyper-IgM syndrome with impaired T-cell dependent B-cell activation and susceptibility to opportunistic infections.
(E) Defective Janus kinase 3 (JAK3) leading to X-linked SCID with absent T cells and NK cells, but normal B cells.

#### Correct Answer: (B)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 3-year-old boy with recurrent severe infections. This age and sex are important for considering X-linked disorders.
- **Infections:** PJP (Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia) and Cryptosporidium diarrhea are classic opportunistic infections seen in severe T-cell immunodeficiencies. PJP specifically points towards a defect in cell-mediated immunity. Cryptosporidium is also a common pathogen in T-cell defects.
- **Vaccination History:** Never received vaccinations, suggesting a chronic underlying condition preventing immunization.
- **Physical Exam:** Lymphopenia (low lymphocyte count) and hepatosplenomegaly are common findings in SCID.
- **Bone Marrow Biopsy:** Reduced T lymphocytes, normal B lymphocytes, and myeloid cells. This points towards a primary T-cell defect, sparing B-cell and myeloid lineages.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Lack of CD3+ (pan-T cell marker), CD4+ (helper T cells), and CD8+ (cytotoxic T cells). Normal CD19+ (B cell marker). This confirms a severe defect in T-cell development/maturation, with preserved B-cell numbers.
- **Graft-versus-Host Disease (GVHD):** Development of severe GVHD after maternal blood transfusion is a hallmark of SCID. The transfused maternal T cells recognize the patient's tissues as foreign and mount an immune attack, which is normally prevented by the patient's own functional T cells. The severity of GVHD strongly suggests a profound lack of the patient's own T cells.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the most likely underlying defect.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **T-cell Development:** T cells develop in the thymus from hematopoietic stem cells. Key steps involve rearrangement of T-cell receptor (TCR) genes, selection processes (positive and negative), and maturation into CD4+ helper T cells and CD8+ cytotoxic T cells.
- **IL-2Rγc (CD132):** The interleukin-2 receptor gamma chain (encoded by the IL2RG gene on the X chromosome) is a common component of receptors for several cytokines crucial for lymphocyte development and function, including IL-2, IL-4, IL-7, IL-9, IL-15, and IL-21. IL-7 signaling via the IL-7R (which includes IL-2Rγc) is essential for T-cell and NK-cell development in the bone marrow and thymus.
- **X-linked SCID (X-SCID):** Mutations in the IL2RG gene cause X-SCID, the most common form of SCID. The defect leads to impaired signaling through multiple cytokine receptors, resulting in a block in lymphocyte development. Specifically, T cells and NK cells are severely deficient or absent, while B cells are usually present but non-functional due to lack of T-cell help.
- **Pathophysiology of X-SCID:** The lack of functional IL-2Rγc prevents proper signaling required for T-cell and NK-cell maturation. B-cell development is less dependent on this pathway, so B cells are typically present, although their function (antibody production, class switching) is impaired due to the absence of T-cell help.
- **Clinical Manifestations of X-SCID:** Patients present early in life (usually within the first few months) with severe, recurrent opportunistic infections (PJP, CMV, Candida, Cryptosporidium), failure to thrive, chronic diarrhea, and often develop severe GVHD after receiving blood products from immunocompetent donors.
- **Diagnosis of X-SCID:** Flow cytometry showing absent or severely reduced T cells and NK cells, with normal or near-normal B-cell numbers. Genetic testing confirms mutations in the IL2RG gene.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Defective RAG 1/2:** RAG1 and RAG2 enzymes are essential for V(D)J recombination, the process that generates diversity in T-cell receptors (TCRs) and B-cell receptors (immunoglobulins). Defects in RAG1/2 lead to SCID characterized by the absence of both T cells and B cells (T-B-NK+ SCID). This contradicts the vignette's finding of normal B-cell numbers.
- (C) **Defective ADA:** Adenosine deaminase (ADA) is an enzyme involved in purine metabolism. Deficiency leads to the accumulation of toxic metabolites (deoxyadenosine triphosphate) that are particularly toxic to lymphocytes, causing a T-B-NK- SCID phenotype (absence of T, B, and NK cells). This contradicts the vignette's finding of normal B-cell numbers and presence of NK cells (implied by the T-B-NK+ phenotype).
- (D) **Defective CD40L:** CD40 ligand (CD40L, encoded by the XLA gene) is expressed on activated T cells and is required for interaction with CD40 on B cells, antigen-presenting cells (APCs), and other cells. Defects in CD40L cause X-linked hyper-IgM syndrome (HIGM1). Patients have normal T-cell numbers and function but impaired T-cell dependent B-cell activation, leading to low levels of IgG, IgA, and IgE, but normal or high levels of IgM. They are susceptible to opportunistic infections, but the primary defect is in B-cell function, not T-cell development, and they do not typically develop severe GVHD after transfusion because their T cells are functional. The vignette describes absent T cells, not impaired B-cell function with normal T cells.
- (E) **Defective JAK3:** Janus kinase 3 (JAK3) is a tyrosine kinase associated with the cytoplasmic tails of several cytokine receptors, including the IL-2Rγc. It is crucial for signal transduction downstream of these receptors. Defects in JAK3 cause an autosomal recessive form of SCID with a phenotype similar to X-SCID (T-B+NK- SCID): absent T cells and NK cells, but normal B-cell numbers. While the T-cell and B-cell numbers match the vignette, the absence of NK cells is a key distinguishing feature of JAK3 deficiency, whereas X-SCID typically involves absent T and NK cells. However, the question asks for the *most likely* defect given the clinical picture, and X-SCID (due to IL2RG mutation) is the most common cause of SCID presenting with absent T cells and normal B cells, especially in a male infant. The severe GVHD is also highly characteristic of X-SCID. While JAK3 deficiency fits the T and B cell numbers, the lack of NK cells is a differentiating feature not explicitly mentioned but often associated with JAK3 deficiency. Given the classic presentation and the X-linked nature often implied by male presentation, IL2RG defect is the most probable.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature             | X-SCID (IL2RG defect) | RAG Deficiency | ADA Deficiency | CD40L Deficiency (HIGM1) | JAK3 Deficiency |
|----------------------|-----------------------|----------------|---------------|--------------------------|-----------------|
| Inheritance         | X-linked              | Autosomal Recessive | Autosomal Recessive | X-linked                 | Autosomal Recessive |
| T Cells             | Absent/Severe ↓       | Absent         | Absent        | Normal                   | Absent          |
| B Cells             | Normal/↓ Function     | Absent         | Absent        | Normal/↓ Function        | Normal          |
| NK Cells            | Absent/Severe ↓       | Normal         | Normal        | Normal                   | Absent          |
| Key Infections      | PJP, CMV, Cryptosporidium | PJP, CMV, Cryptosporidium | PJP, CMV, Cryptosporidium | PJP, CMV, Cryptosporidium | PJP, CMV, Cryptosporidium |
| GVHD                | Severe                | Severe         | Severe        | Less common              | Severe          |
| Primary Defect      | IL-2Rγc signaling     | V(D)J Recombination | Purine Metabolism | T-B cell interaction     | JAK3 signaling  |
| Flow Cytometry      | CD3-, CD4-, CD8- low, CD19+ normal | CD3-, CD4-, CD8-, CD19- | CD3-, CD4-, CD8-, CD19-, CD16/56- | CD3+ normal, CD19+ normal | CD3-, CD4-, CD8- low, CD19+ normal, CD16/56- |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The classic presentation of a young male with severe opportunistic infections (PJP, Cryptosporidium), failure to thrive, lymphopenia, normal B-cell numbers but absent T cells, and severe GVHD after transfusion strongly points towards X-linked Severe Combined Immunodeficiency (X-SCID) due to a defect in the IL-2Rγc (CD132). Differentiating SCID subtypes requires careful analysis of lymphocyte subsets (T, B, NK cells) via flow cytometry and understanding the underlying molecular defects. Remember that RAG deficiency affects both T and B cells, ADA deficiency affects all lymphocytes, CD40L deficiency primarily affects B-cell function with normal T cells, and JAK3 deficiency affects T and NK cells but spares B cells.

<a id='question-147'></a>

### Question 147: Immunodeficiency & Opportunistic Infection
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of specific immunodeficiencies, particularly combined immunodeficiencies affecting both T and B cell function, and their association with specific opportunistic infections. It also assesses the ability to interpret clinical presentation, laboratory findings (specifically lymphocyte subset analysis), and link them to the underlying immunological defect. (考察特定免疫缺陷，特别是影响T细胞和B细胞功能的联合免疫缺陷，及其与特定机会性感染的关联。同时评估解读临床表现、实验室发现（特别是淋巴细胞亚群分析）并将其与潜在的免疫缺陷联系起来的能力。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 3-year-old boy presents with recurrent severe bacterial infections (pneumonia, otitis media, sinusitis) starting at 6 months of age, failure to thrive, chronic diarrhea, and persistent oral thrush. He has never received any vaccinations. Physical examination reveals lymphadenopathy and hepatosplenomegaly. Flow cytometry analysis of peripheral blood lymphocytes shows markedly reduced numbers of CD3+ T cells, CD4+ T cells, and CD8+ T cells, along with significantly decreased CD19+ B cells and CD56+ NK cells. Immunoglobulin levels (IgG, IgA, IgM) are all severely reduced. Despite broad-spectrum antibiotic therapy, he develops disseminated *Pneumocystis jirovecii* pneumonia (PJP) and cytomegalovirus (CMV) retinitis.

Which of the following is the most likely underlying immunological defect in this patient?

(A) X-linked agammaglobulinemia (XLA)
(B) Severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID) due to adenosine deaminase (ADA) deficiency
(C) DiGeorge syndrome (22q11.2 deletion syndrome)
(D) Common variable immunodeficiency (CVID)
(E) Chronic granulomatous disease (CGD)

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Age of onset (6 months):** Suggests a congenital immunodeficiency, as maternal antibodies wane around this time.
*   **Recurrent severe bacterial infections:** Indicates a defect in humoral immunity (B cells/antibodies) and/or cell-mediated immunity (T cells).
*   **Failure to thrive, chronic diarrhea, oral thrush:** Common features of severe immunodeficiency, often due to opportunistic infections (fungal, viral, bacterial).
*   **Lack of vaccinations:** Consistent with a severe immunodeficiency preventing effective immune response to vaccines.
*   **Lymphadenopathy and hepatosplenomegaly:** Can be seen in various immunodeficiencies, sometimes due to chronic infections or immune dysregulation.
*   **Flow cytometry:** Shows profound reduction in *all* major lymphocyte lineages: T cells (CD3+, CD4+, CD8+), B cells (CD19+), and NK cells (CD56+). This points towards a severe defect affecting lymphocyte development or survival.
*   **Severely reduced immunoglobulin levels:** Confirms a defect in B cell function or development, leading to impaired antibody production.
*   **Disseminated *Pneumocystis jirovecii* pneumonia (PJP) and CMV retinitis:** These are classic opportunistic infections seen in patients with severe T cell deficiency. PJP is particularly characteristic of profound T cell defects.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
*   **Severe Combined Immunodeficiency (SCID):** SCID is a group of genetic disorders characterized by profound defects in both T cell and B cell function, leading to extreme susceptibility to infections early in life. The flow cytometry findings (panlymphopenia affecting T, B, and NK cells) and the clinical presentation (early onset, severe bacterial, fungal, and viral infections, including PJP and CMV) are classic for SCID.
*   **Adenosine Deaminase (ADA) Deficiency:** ADA is an enzyme crucial for purine metabolism in lymphocytes. Deficiency leads to the accumulation of toxic metabolites (deoxyadenosine triphosphate, dATP), which inhibit DNA synthesis and repair, causing lymphocyte apoptosis, particularly affecting T cells and B cells, resulting in SCID. This is one of the most common genetic causes of SCID.
*   **X-linked Agammaglobulinemia (XLA):** Caused by mutations in the *BTK* gene, leading to a block in B cell development at the pre-B cell stage. Patients have absent B cells and immunoglobulins, but T cell numbers and function are normal. They are susceptible to encapsulated bacteria but typically do not have severe viral or fungal infections like PJP or CMV. Flow cytometry would show absent CD19+ B cells but normal T cells.
*   **DiGeorge Syndrome:** Caused by a 22q11.2 deletion, affecting the development of the thymus and parathyroid glands. Patients have variable T cell deficiency (ranging from mild to severe) due to thymic hypoplasia/aplasia, often associated with hypocalcemia. B cell numbers are usually normal, although antibody responses can be impaired. Flow cytometry typically shows reduced CD3+ T cells (especially CD4+), but B cells and NK cells are usually present. PJP is less common than in classic SCID.
*   **Common Variable Immunodeficiency (CVID):** A primary immunodeficiency characterized by low immunoglobulin levels and impaired antibody responses, typically presenting later in life (childhood or adulthood). B cell numbers are usually normal or slightly reduced, but they fail to differentiate into plasma cells. T cell numbers are generally normal, although T cell function can be impaired in some patients. Patients are susceptible to recurrent bacterial infections, but severe opportunistic infections like PJP and CMV are less common than in SCID.
*   **Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD):** A defect in phagocyte function (neutrophils, macrophages) due to mutations in genes encoding components of the NADPH oxidase complex. Phagocytes cannot produce superoxide radicals needed to kill certain catalase-positive bacteria and fungi. Patients have recurrent bacterial and fungal infections (e.g., *Staphylococcus aureus*, *Aspergillus*) forming granulomas. Lymphocyte numbers and function are normal. Flow cytometry would not show panlymphopenia.

The patient's presentation with early onset, severe infections (bacterial, fungal - PJP, viral - CMV), failure to thrive, and flow cytometry showing profound deficiency of T cells, B cells, and NK cells is pathognomonic for SCID. ADA deficiency is a common cause of this phenotype.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **X-linked agammaglobulinemia (XLA):** Incorrect. XLA primarily affects B cell development, leading to absent B cells and antibodies. T cell numbers and function are normal. Patients are susceptible to bacterial infections but typically lack severe viral/fungal infections like PJP and CMV, and flow cytometry would show normal T cells. The panlymphopenia seen in the vignette rules out XLA.
- (C) **DiGeorge syndrome (22q11.2 deletion syndrome):** Incorrect. DiGeorge syndrome involves T cell deficiency due to thymic hypoplasia, but B cell numbers are usually normal. The severity of T cell deficiency is variable, and panlymphopenia (including B and NK cells) is not typical. While patients can get opportunistic infections, the profound deficiency across all lymphocyte lineages points away from DiGeorge.
- (D) **Common variable immunodeficiency (CVID):** Incorrect. CVID typically presents later in life and is characterized by low immunoglobulins and impaired antibody responses, but B cell numbers are usually normal or only slightly reduced, and T cell numbers are generally normal. Severe opportunistic infections like PJP and CMV are uncommon. The early onset and panlymphopenia are inconsistent with CVID.
- (E) **Chronic granulomatous disease (CGD):** Incorrect. CGD is a phagocyte defect, not a lymphocyte defect. Lymphocyte counts and function are normal. Patients have recurrent bacterial and fungal infections but do not typically present with panlymphopenia or severe viral infections like CMV. Flow cytometry would be normal for lymphocytes.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | SCID (e.g., ADA def.) | XLA                  | DiGeorge Syndrome    | CVID                 | CGD                  |
| :---------------------- | :------------------- | :------------------- | :------------------- | :------------------- | :------------------- |
| **Onset**               | Early (infancy)      | Early (infancy)      | Early (infancy)      | Later (child/adult)  | Early (infancy)      |
| **T Cells (CD3+)**      | Severely Reduced    | Normal               | Reduced (variable)   | Normal               | Normal               |
| **B Cells (CD19+)**     | Severely Reduced    | Absent               | Normal               | Normal/Reduced       | Normal               |
| **NK Cells (CD56+)**    | Severely Reduced    | Normal               | Normal               | Normal               | Normal               |
| **Immunoglobulins**     | Severely Reduced    | Severely Reduced    | Normal/Reduced       | Severely Reduced    | Normal               |
| **PJP/CMV**             | Common               | Rare                 | Possible (if severe T cell defect) | Rare                 | Rare                 |
| **Primary Defect**      | Lymphocyte Dev/Survival | B Cell Development   | Thymus Development   | B Cell Differentiation | Phagocyte Function   |
| **Flow Cytometry**      | Panlymphopenia       | Absent B cells       | Reduced T cells      | Normal B cells       | Normal Lymphocytes   |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
SCID is characterized by profound defects in both T and B cell immunity, leading to severe opportunistic infections early in life. Flow cytometry showing panlymphopenia (reduced T, B, and NK cells) is a key diagnostic feature. Differentiating SCID from other primary immunodeficiencies like XLA (B cell defect only), DiGeorge (T cell defect, normal B cells), CVID (late onset, normal T cells), and CGD (phagocyte defect) relies on understanding the specific lymphocyte populations affected and the typical infections seen.

<a id='question-148'></a>

### Question 148: Immunodeficiency & Opportunistic Infection
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of specific immunodeficiencies, particularly combined immunodeficiencies affecting both T and B cell function, and their susceptibility to specific opportunistic infections. It requires differentiating between various primary immunodeficiencies based on clinical presentation and pathogen characteristics. (本题考察特定免疫缺陷，特别是影响T细胞和B细胞功能的联合免疫缺陷，以及它们对特定机会性感染的易感性。需要根据临床表现和病原体特征区分各种原发性免疫缺陷。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 3-year-old boy, previously healthy, presents with recurrent severe bacterial infections (pneumonia, otitis media, sinusitis) starting at 6 months of age, failure to thrive, and chronic diarrhea. He has never received any vaccinations. Physical examination reveals lymphadenopathy and hepatosplenomegaly. Laboratory studies show markedly reduced numbers of peripheral blood lymphocytes (absolute lymphocyte count 500/µL), absent T cell-mediated delayed-type hypersensitivity (DTH) skin reactions to Candida albicans and tuberculin, and severely decreased serum IgG, IgA, and IgM levels. Flow cytometry reveals absent CD3+ T cells and absent CD19+ B cells, but normal numbers of CD14+ monocytes and CD16+ NK cells. A bone marrow biopsy shows markedly reduced cellularity with a paucity of lymphoid precursors. The patient is subsequently diagnosed with severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID). Which of the following pathogens is MOST likely to cause a severe, life-threatening infection in this patient?

(A) *Pneumocystis jirovecii*
(B) *Staphylococcus aureus*
(C) *Candida albicans*
(D) *Mycobacterium tuberculosis*
(E) *Aspergillus fumigatus*

#### Correct Answer: (A)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 3-year-old boy with recurrent severe infections starting at 6 months (when maternal antibodies wane). This timing is classic for primary immunodeficiencies affecting antibody production or T cell function.
- **Infection Types:** Recurrent severe bacterial infections (pneumonia, otitis, sinusitis) suggest impaired humoral immunity (B cells/antibodies) or phagocyte function. Chronic diarrhea points towards potential T cell defects or specific enteric pathogens.
- **Physical Exam:** Lymphadenopathy and hepatosplenomegaly can be seen in various immunodeficiencies, infections, or malignancies.
- **Laboratory Findings:**
    - Markedly reduced lymphocytes (lymphopenia) is a key feature of severe immunodeficiencies.
    - Absent DTH reactions indicate defective T cell function (specifically, T helper cell function required for macrophage activation).
    - Severely decreased IgG, IgA, IgM indicates profound B cell dysfunction or lack of T cell help for B cell maturation/antibody production.
    - Flow cytometry: Absent CD3+ T cells and CD19+ B cells confirms a severe defect in both T and B cell lineages. Normal CD14+ monocytes and CD16+ NK cells help narrow the differential.
    - Bone marrow biopsy: Reduced cellularity with paucity of lymphoid precursors suggests a defect in lymphocyte development or survival.
- **Diagnosis:** Severe Combined Immunodeficiency (SCID) is confirmed by the constellation of findings: severe lymphopenia, absent T cells, absent B cells, absent DTH, and panhypogammaglobulinemia.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the MOST likely pathogen to cause a *severe, life-threatening* infection in a patient with SCID. This requires knowledge of opportunistic infections associated with specific immune defects.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **SCID Pathophysiology:** SCID represents a group of genetic disorders characterized by profound defects in both T and B lymphocyte development and/or function. This leads to severe susceptibility to a wide range of infections, including bacterial, viral, fungal, and protozoal pathogens. The absence of T cells is particularly critical, as T cells are essential for cell-mediated immunity (killing intracellular pathogens) and for providing help to B cells for antibody production.
- **Opportunistic Infections in SCID:** Patients with SCID are highly susceptible to opportunistic infections that are typically controlled by a competent immune system.
    - *Pneumocystis jirovecii* (formerly *Pneumocystis carinii*) is a fungus that causes *Pneumocystis* pneumonia (PCP), a severe and often fatal lung infection, particularly in individuals with impaired T cell immunity (e.g., HIV/AIDS, SCID). It is a classic opportunistic pathogen in T cell deficiencies.
    - *Staphylococcus aureus* is a common cause of bacterial infections, including pneumonia, sepsis, and skin infections. While SCID patients are susceptible to bacterial infections due to lack of antibodies and impaired phagocyte activation (due to lack of T cell help), *S. aureus* is not typically considered the *most* life-threatening opportunistic pathogen specifically associated with the profound T cell defect seen in SCID compared to organisms like *Pneumocystis*.
    - *Candida albicans* is a common commensal yeast that can cause opportunistic infections (candidiasis), especially in immunocompromised individuals. While SCID patients are susceptible, severe, disseminated candidiasis is less characteristic than PCP in this context.
    - *Mycobacterium tuberculosis* causes tuberculosis. While T cell immunity is crucial for controlling *M. tuberculosis*, disseminated BCG infection (from the attenuated vaccine strain) is a more common severe manifestation in SCID than infection with wild-type *M. tuberculosis*.
    - *Aspergillus fumigatus* is a mold that can cause invasive aspergillosis, particularly in patients with neutropenia or impaired T cell function. While possible, PCP is a more frequent and characteristic severe opportunistic infection in SCID.
- **Conclusion:** Given the profound T cell defect in SCID, *Pneumocystis jirovecii* is a major opportunistic pathogen known to cause severe, life-threatening pneumonia in these patients.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) ***Pneumocystis jirovecii***: **Correct.** This fungus is a classic opportunistic pathogen causing severe pneumonia (PCP) in patients with profound T cell immunodeficiency, such as SCID. The lack of T cell-mediated immunity is critical for controlling *Pneumocystis* proliferation in the lungs.
- (B) ***Staphylococcus aureus***: **Incorrect.** While SCID patients are susceptible to bacterial infections due to impaired antibody production and potentially impaired phagocyte function (lack of T cell help), *S. aureus* is a common pathogen causing infections in many immunocompromised states, not specifically the *most* characteristic severe opportunistic infection linked to the profound T cell defect of SCID compared to *Pneumocystis*.
- (C) ***Candida albicans***: **Incorrect.** *Candida* infections are common in immunocompromised hosts, including SCID patients. However, severe, disseminated candidiasis is less frequently the *most* life-threatening initial opportunistic infection compared to PCP in SCID.
- (D) ***Mycobacterium tuberculosis***: **Incorrect.** Patients with SCID are susceptible to mycobacterial infections, but disseminated infection with the attenuated BCG vaccine strain is a more characteristic severe manifestation in SCID than infection with wild-type *M. tuberculosis*. Wild-type *M. tuberculosis* infection is more associated with defects in macrophage function or specific T cell responses (like IFN-gamma).
- (E) ***Aspergillus fumigatus***: **Incorrect.** Invasive aspergillosis is typically seen in patients with neutropenia or severe T cell defects. While possible in SCID, *Pneumocystis* pneumonia is a more common and defining severe opportunistic infection associated with the profound T cell deficiency characteristic of SCID.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Pathogen/Condition        | SCID Susceptibility | Key Immune Defect | Typical Manifestation in SCID |
|---------------------------|----------------------|-------------------|-----------------------------|
| *Pneumocystis jirovecii* | High                 | T cell deficiency | Severe Pneumonia (PCP)      |
| *Staphylococcus aureus*   | High                 | B cell/T cell help | Sepsis, Pneumonia           |
| *Candida albicans*        | High                 | T cell/B cell help | Thrush, Esophagitis, Sepsis |
| *Mycobacterium tuberculosis*| High                 | T cell deficiency | Disseminated BCG infection  |
| *Aspergillus fumigatus*   | High                 | T cell deficiency | Invasive Aspergillosis      |
| X-linked Agammaglobulinemia| High                 | B cell deficiency | Encapsulated bacteria       |
| DiGeorge Syndrome         | Variable             | T cell deficiency | Viral, Fungal infections    |
| Chronic Granulomatous Disease| High                 | Phagocyte defect  | Catalase-positive organisms |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
SCID patients lack functional T and B cells, making them highly susceptible to a wide range of opportunistic infections. *Pneumocystis jirovecii* pneumonia (PCP) is a hallmark severe opportunistic infection in patients with profound T cell deficiency, such as SCID. Differentiating between opportunistic infections based on the specific immune defect is crucial for diagnosis and management. Key differentials for severe infections in SCID include bacterial sepsis, disseminated viral infections (e.g., CMV, VZV), and severe fungal infections like *Candida* or *Aspergillus*. However, PCP is particularly characteristic due to the critical role of T cells in controlling this organism.

<a id='question-149'></a>

### Question 149: Immunodeficiency & Opportunistic Infection
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of specific immunodeficiencies, particularly combined immunodeficiencies affecting both T and B cell function, and their susceptibility to specific opportunistic infections. It also assesses the ability to interpret flow cytometry data to identify deficient lymphocyte populations. (本题考察特定免疫缺陷的理解，特别是影响 T 细胞和 B 细胞功能的联合免疫缺陷，以及它们对特定机会性感染的易感性。它还评估了利用流式细胞术数据识别缺乏的淋巴细胞群体的能力。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 3-year-old boy presents with recurrent severe bacterial infections (pneumonia, otitis media, sinusitis) starting at 6 months of age, failure to thrive, and chronic diarrhea. He has never received any vaccinations. Physical examination reveals lymphadenopathy and hepatosplenomegaly. Flow cytometry analysis of peripheral blood lymphocytes shows markedly reduced numbers of CD3+ T cells, CD4+ T cells, and CD8+ T cells, along with significantly decreased numbers of CD19+ B cells and CD16+/CD56+ NK cells. Immunoglobulin levels (IgG, IgA, IgM) are severely reduced. The patient is subsequently diagnosed with Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia (PJP).

Which of the following immunological defects is most likely responsible for this patient's presentation and susceptibility to PJP?

(A) X-linked agammaglobulinemia (XLA)
(B) DiGeorge syndrome
(C) Severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID)
(D) Common variable immunodeficiency (CVID)
(E) Chronic granulomatous disease (CGD)

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Age of onset (6 months):** Suggests a congenital immunodeficiency, as maternal antibodies wane around this time.
*   **Recurrent severe bacterial infections:** Indicates a defect in humoral immunity (B cells/antibodies) and/or cellular immunity (T cells).
*   **Failure to thrive, chronic diarrhea:** Common features of severe immunodeficiency.
*   **Lack of vaccinations:** Consistent with a severe immunodeficiency preventing effective immune response to vaccines.
*   **Lymphadenopathy and hepatosplenomegaly:** Can be seen in various immunodeficiencies, sometimes due to chronic infections or immune dysregulation.
*   **Flow cytometry:** Shows profound reduction in *all* major lymphocyte lineages: T cells (CD3+, CD4+, CD8+), B cells (CD19+), and NK cells (CD16+/CD56+). This points to a severe defect affecting lymphocyte development or survival.
*   **Severely reduced immunoglobulin levels:** Confirms a defect in B cell function or development.
*   **Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia (PJP):** A classic opportunistic infection associated with severe T cell deficiency.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
*   **Severe Combined Immunodeficiency (SCID):** A group of genetic disorders characterized by profound defects in both T cell and B cell function, often affecting NK cells as well. This results from mutations in genes crucial for lymphocyte development or signaling. The lack of functional T cells impairs cell-mediated immunity (necessary for controlling intracellular pathogens like *Pneumocystis jirovecii*) and T cell help for B cells (leading to antibody deficiency). The flow cytometry findings (pan-lymphopenia affecting T, B, and NK cells) and the clinical presentation (early onset, severe recurrent infections, failure to thrive, PJP) are classic for SCID.
*   **X-linked agammaglobulinemia (XLA):** Caused by mutations in the *BTK* gene, leading to a block in B cell development. Patients have absent B cells and immunoglobulins, resulting in susceptibility to encapsulated bacteria. T cell numbers and function are normal. This doesn't fit the pan-lymphopenia and T cell deficiency seen here.
*   **DiGeorge syndrome:** Caused by a deletion on chromosome 22q11.2, leading to thymic hypoplasia/aplasia (affecting T cell development) and parathyroid hypoplasia (causing hypocalcemia). Patients have variable T cell deficiency, often with normal or near-normal B cell numbers and immunoglobulin levels (though antibody responses may be impaired). The severe pan-lymphopenia seen in the vignette is not typical.
*   **Common variable immunodeficiency (CVID):** Characterized by low immunoglobulin levels and impaired antibody responses, usually presenting later in life (adolescence or adulthood). B cell numbers are typically normal or slightly reduced, but they fail to differentiate into plasma cells. T cell numbers are usually normal, although T cell function can be impaired in some patients. The early onset and severe T cell deficiency rule out CVID.
*   **Chronic granulomatous disease (CGD):** A defect in phagocyte NADPH oxidase, impairing the ability of neutrophils and macrophages to kill certain bacteria and fungi via oxidative burst. Patients have recurrent infections with catalase-positive organisms (e.g., *Staphylococcus aureus*, *Aspergillus*). Lymphocyte numbers and function are normal. This doesn't fit the clinical picture or flow cytometry results.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **X-linked agammaglobulinemia (XLA):** Incorrect. XLA primarily affects B cell development, leading to absent B cells and antibodies. T cell numbers and function are normal. The patient in the vignette has severe T cell deficiency (CD3+, CD4+, CD8+ cells are markedly reduced) and pan-lymphopenia, which is inconsistent with XLA. XLA patients are susceptible to encapsulated bacteria but typically not PJP, which requires T cell immunity.
- (B) **DiGeorge syndrome:** Incorrect. DiGeorge syndrome results from thymic hypoplasia/aplasia, causing T cell deficiency. However, B cell numbers are usually normal or near-normal, and NK cells are typically present. The vignette describes severe reduction in *all* lymphocyte lineages (T, B, NK), which is not characteristic of DiGeorge syndrome.
- (D) **Common variable immunodeficiency (CVID):** Incorrect. CVID is characterized by hypogammaglobulinemia and impaired antibody responses, typically presenting later in life. T cell numbers are usually normal, although function may be affected. The early onset (6 months), severe pan-lymphopenia, and profound T cell deficiency seen in the vignette are inconsistent with CVID.
- (E) **Chronic granulomatous disease (CGD):** Incorrect. CGD is a phagocyte defect, not a lymphocyte defect. Patients have normal lymphocyte counts and function but are susceptible to infections with catalase-positive organisms due to impaired intracellular killing by phagocytes. The vignette describes severe lymphopenia and susceptibility to PJP, which is inconsistent with CGD.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | SCID (Correct)                                  | XLA (A)                                     | DiGeorge (B)                               | CVID (D)                                    | CGD (E)                                    |
| :---------------------- | :---------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------ | :----------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------ | :----------------------------------------- |
| **Primary Defect**      | Lymphocyte development/function (T, B, +/- NK) | B cell development                          | T cell development (thymic hypoplasia)      | B cell differentiation (plasma cells)       | Phagocyte oxidative burst                  |
| **T Cells (CD3+)**      | Severely reduced/absent                        | Normal                                      | Reduced (variable)                          | Normal                                      | Normal                                      |
| **B Cells (CD19+)**     | Severely reduced/absent                        | Absent                                      | Normal/Near Normal                         | Normal/Slightly reduced                    | Normal                                      |
| **NK Cells (CD16+/56+)** | Often reduced/absent                           | Normal                                      | Normal                                      | Normal                                      | Normal                                      |
| **Immunoglobulins**     | Severely reduced/absent                        | Severely reduced/absent                     | Normal/Near Normal                         | Severely reduced/absent                     | Normal                                      |
| **Age of Onset**        | Early (infancy)                                 | Early (infancy)                             | Variable (infancy/childhood)               | Later (adolescence/adulthood)               | Early (infancy/childhood)                  |
| **Typical Infections**  | Severe bacterial, viral, fungal, PJP            | Encapsulated bacteria                       | Viral, fungal (if T cell defect severe)     | Encapsulated bacteria                      | Catalase-positive bacteria, fungi           |
| **Flow Cytometry**      | Pan-lymphopenia (T, B, NK)                      | Absent B cells, Normal T/NK cells            | Reduced T cells, Normal B/NK cells          | Normal T/NK cells, Normal/Low B cells       | Normal lymphocytes                          |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
SCID is characterized by severe defects in both T and B cell immunity, often affecting NK cells as well, leading to pan-lymphopenia and susceptibility to a wide range of opportunistic infections, including PJP, early in life. Differentiating SCID from other primary immunodeficiencies like XLA (B cell defect only), DiGeorge (T cell defect only), CVID (late-onset antibody defect), and CGD (phagocyte defect) requires careful consideration of the specific lymphocyte populations affected and the types of infections encountered.

<a id='question-150'></a>

### Question 150: The Immune System: Part 3 Focus 50 (contain)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of complement components, specifically C3 convertases (C4b2a and C3bBb), in opsonization and lysis, and how deficiencies or dysregulation can lead to increased susceptibility to infections, particularly encapsulated bacteria. (理解补体成分，特别是C3转化酶（C4b2a和C3bBb）在吞噬作用和溶血中的作用，以及缺陷或失调如何导致对感染的易感性增加，特别是对荚膜细菌。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 2-year-old boy with a history of recurrent, severe bacterial infections (including *Neisseria meningitidis* meningitis at age 1 and *Streptococcus pneumoniae* pneumonia at age 1.5) is brought to the emergency department with fever, chills, and a purpuric rash. His mother reports that he recently received a meningococcal vaccine, but developed a severe systemic reaction shortly after injection, including urticaria, angioedema, and difficulty breathing. Physical examination reveals a petechial rash on his trunk and extremities, and he appears septic. Laboratory studies show markedly decreased serum levels of C3 and C4, normal C1q, C1r, C1s, C5-C9, and properdin levels, and a normal alternative pathway (measured by AH50). A peripheral blood smear shows no abnormalities in leukocyte counts or morphology. Given his history and current presentation, which of the following is the most likely underlying defect?

(A) Deficiency of Factor H
(B) Deficiency of C5
(C) Deficiency of C4b-binding protein (C4BP)
(D) Deficiency of Properdin
(E) Deficiency of C3

#### Correct Answer: (C)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Recurrent severe bacterial infections:** Suggests an underlying immunodeficiency, particularly affecting opsonization or complement-mediated killing, which are crucial for defense against encapsulated bacteria like *N. meningitidis* and *S. pneumoniae*.
*   **Specific pathogens (*N. meningitidis*, *S. pneumoniae*):** These are classic encapsulated bacteria whose clearance relies heavily on complement activation (especially the classical and alternative pathways leading to C3b opsonization and MAC formation) and antibody-mediated responses.
*   **Severe reaction to meningococcal vaccine:** This suggests a potential issue with the classical complement pathway, as meningococcal vaccines often contain components that can activate this pathway. Severe reactions like anaphylaxis or serum sickness can occur in individuals with complement deficiencies, particularly C1-C4 deficiencies, due to impaired clearance of immune complexes.
*   **Petechial/purpuric rash:** Suggests disseminated intravascular coagulation (DIC) or vasculitis, potentially triggered by severe infection or immune complex deposition.
*   **Markedly decreased serum C3 and C4:** This is the most critical clue. Low C3 indicates a problem downstream of C3 activation (either classical or alternative pathway). Low C4 specifically points towards a defect in the classical pathway *before* C3 activation, or a defect affecting C4 stability.
*   **Normal C1q, C1r, C1s, C5-C9, Properdin, and AH50:** Rules out deficiencies in the early classical pathway components (C1q, C1r, C1s), the terminal complement pathway (C5-C9), and the alternative pathway (indicated by normal AH50, which measures the integrity of the alternative pathway).
*   **Normal leukocyte counts/morphology:** Rules out primary hematologic disorders or severe neutropenia as the cause of recurrent infections.

Combining these clues: The patient has recurrent infections with encapsulated bacteria, a severe reaction to a vaccine likely activating the classical pathway, and markedly low C3 and C4 levels, while early classical components (C1q, C1r, C1s) and the alternative pathway (Properdin, AH50) are normal. This pattern strongly suggests a deficiency in a component that regulates the classical pathway *after* C1 activation but *before* C3 activation, or a defect affecting C4 stability.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
*   **Complement Pathways:** The complement system has three activation pathways: classical, alternative, and lectin. All converge at the formation of C3 convertase.
    *   **Classical Pathway:** Activated by antigen-antibody complexes (IgG, IgM) or directly by certain pathogens. Involves C1q, C1r, C1s, C4, and C2. Forms the C3 convertase C4b2a.
    *   **Alternative Pathway:** Activated spontaneously on microbial surfaces or by certain pathogens. Involves Factor B, Factor D, Properdin, and C3. Forms the C3 convertase C3bBb.
    *   **Lectin Pathway:** Activated by mannose-binding lectin (MBL) binding to microbial carbohydrates. Similar to the classical pathway, it uses MASPs (MBL-associated serine proteases) and C4/C2 to form C4b2a.
*   **C3 Convertases:** C4b2a (classical/lectin) and C3bBb (alternative) cleave C3 into C3a (anaphylatoxin) and C3b (opsonin, component of C5 convertase).
*   **C5 Convertases:** C4b2a3b (classical/lectin) and C3bBb3b (alternative) cleave C5 into C5a (anaphylatoxin, chemoattractant) and C5b, initiating the terminal pathway (C5b-9, the Membrane Attack Complex - MAC).
*   **Regulation of Complement:** The complement system is tightly regulated to prevent damage to host tissues. Key regulators include:
    *   **C1 Inhibitor (C1-INH):** Inhibits C1r, C1s, MASPs, Factor D, and kallikrein. Deficiency leads to hereditary angioedema (HAE) and increased susceptibility to infections.
    *   **Factor H:** Regulates the alternative pathway by accelerating the decay of C3bBb and acting as a cofactor for Factor I-mediated cleavage of C3b. Deficiency leads to atypical hemolytic uremic syndrome (aHUS) and increased susceptibility to *Neisseria* infections.
    *   **C4b-binding protein (C4BP):** Binds to C4b and accelerates its decay, preventing excessive classical/lectin pathway activation. It also binds to C1q and C1-INH, facilitating their interaction. Deficiency leads to increased susceptibility to infections (especially encapsulated bacteria) and autoimmune diseases (like SLE) due to impaired clearance of immune complexes and increased inflammation.
    *   **Decay-Accelerating Factor (DAF/CD55):** Accelerates the decay of C3 convertases (C4b2a and C3bBb) on host cell surfaces. Deficiency leads to paroxysmal nocturnal hemoglobinuria (PNH).
    *   **Membrane Cofactor Protein (MCP/CD46):** Acts as a cofactor for Factor I-mediated cleavage of C3b and C4b. Deficiency leads to PNH.
*   **Clinical Manifestations of Complement Deficiencies:**
    *   **Early Component Deficiencies (C1q, C1r, C1s, C4, C2):** Increased susceptibility to infections with encapsulated bacteria (especially *S. pneumoniae*, *H. influenzae*, *N. meningitidis*). Increased risk of autoimmune diseases (like SLE) due to impaired clearance of immune complexes and apoptotic debris. Severe reactions to vaccines containing adjuvants that activate the classical pathway.
    *   **C3 Deficiency:** Most severe complement deficiency. Profound susceptibility to recurrent pyogenic infections (especially encapsulated bacteria).
    *   **Late Component Deficiencies (C5-C9):** Increased susceptibility to recurrent infections with *Neisseria* species (meningitis, gonococcal arthritis).
    *   **Alternative Pathway Deficiencies (Factor B, Factor D, Properdin):** Increased susceptibility to *Neisseria* infections.
    *   **Regulatory Protein Deficiencies (Factor H, C4BP, DAF, MCP):** Can lead to autoimmune diseases (SLE, aHUS, PNH) and/or increased susceptibility to infections.

In this case, the low C3 and C4, normal early classical components (C1q, C1r, C1s), and normal alternative pathway (Properdin, AH50) point towards a deficiency in C4 regulation or stability. C4BP deficiency fits this pattern perfectly. It leads to decreased C4 levels (due to accelerated decay) and subsequently decreased C3 levels (due to reduced C3 convertase formation/activity in the classical pathway). The impaired clearance of immune complexes also explains the severe vaccine reaction and increased susceptibility to infections.

##### 3. Linking the Clues to the Correct Answer (整合线索，指向正确答案)
The patient's clinical picture (recurrent encapsulated bacterial infections, severe vaccine reaction) combined with the specific laboratory findings (low C3, low C4, normal C1q/r/s, normal C5-C9, normal Properdin, normal AH50) is characteristic of C4b-binding protein (C4BP) deficiency. This deficiency leads to uncontrolled activation of the classical pathway (due to lack of C4b regulation) and subsequent consumption of C4 and C3.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Deficiency of Factor H:** Factor H regulates the *alternative* pathway. Deficiency would lead to increased alternative pathway activity, low C3, but typically normal C4 levels. It is associated with aHUS and increased susceptibility to *Neisseria* infections, but not typically with low C4 or severe classical pathway vaccine reactions. The normal AH50 in the vignette rules this out.
- (B) **Deficiency of C5:** Deficiency of C5 would impair the formation of the Membrane Attack Complex (MAC), leading to increased susceptibility specifically to *Neisseria* species. It would *not* affect C3 or C4 levels, as C5 is downstream of C3 convertase formation. The vignette shows low C3 and C4, ruling this out.
- (D) **Deficiency of Properdin:** Properdin stabilizes the C3 convertase (C3bBb) of the *alternative* pathway. Deficiency leads to impaired alternative pathway function, low C3, but typically normal C4 levels. It is associated with increased susceptibility to *Neisseria* infections. The normal AH50 and low C4 rule this out.
- (E) **Deficiency of C3:** Deficiency of C3 is the most severe complement deficiency, leading to very low C3 levels and susceptibility to a wide range of bacterial infections. However, C4 levels would typically be normal or even slightly elevated (due to lack of consumption in C3 convertase formation). The vignette specifically shows *low* C4 levels, making C3 deficiency less likely than a defect affecting C4 stability or regulation.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | C4BP Deficiency | Factor H Deficiency | C5 Deficiency | Properdin Deficiency | C3 Deficiency |
| :---------------------- | :-------------- | :------------------ | :------------ | :------------------- | :------------ |
| **Pathway Primarily Affected** | Classical       | Alternative         | Terminal      | Alternative          | All           |
| **C3 Level**            | Low             | Low                 | Normal        | Low                  | Very Low      |
| **C4 Level**            | Low             | Normal              | Normal        | Normal               | Normal/High   |
| **Properdin Level**     | Normal          | Normal              | Normal        | Low                  | Normal        |
| **AH50**                | Normal          | Low                 | Normal        | Low                  | Normal        |
| **Typical Infections**   | Encapsulated bacteria | *Neisseria*         | *Neisseria*   | *Neisseria*          | Wide range    |
| **Autoimmune Risk**     | High (SLE)      | High (aHUS)         | Low           | Low                  | High          |
| **Vaccine Reaction**    | Yes             | No                  | No            | No                   | No            |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Complement deficiencies are a key cause of recurrent bacterial infections, especially with encapsulated organisms. Understanding the specific components involved in each pathway (classical, alternative, terminal) and their regulation is crucial. Low C3 and C4 with normal early classical components and alternative pathway markers strongly suggests a defect in C4 regulation, such as C4BP deficiency. Differentiating complement deficiencies based on component levels (C3, C4, Properdin, CH50) is a high-yield topic for USMLE Step 1. Key differentials include deficiencies in early components (C1q, C2), late components (C5-C9), alternative pathway components (Factor H, Properdin), and regulatory proteins (C1-INH, Factor H, C4BP, DAF, MCP).
